I went to this dealership out on Route 9, and the salesman—this guy in a really loud plaid suit, I swear he looked like he stepped out of the 1970s—he totally fast-talked me into getting the extended warranty and the undercoating and all this stuff I didn't need. I drove off the lot and just felt this immediate wave of regret. Like, I knew better, you know? I have a degree. I shouldn't be falling for those kinds of tricks. But he just kept talking and talking, kind of like how my mom gets when she calls on Sundays. She'll just go on for an hour about her neighbor's dog digging up her petunias, and I can't get a word in edgewise. Have you ever tried growing petunias? I hear they're really finicky. But yeah, the guilt is definitely there. It's really overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: Do you enjoy the food or feel any pleasure while you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if I enjoy it. I mean, maybe for the first bite or two? But then it just turns into this frantic thing, and I'm not even tasting it anymore. It's just... I don't know, it's not about pleasure. \n\nIt's actually a lot like when I used to go to these all-you-can-eat buffets with my roommate back in college. We'd go to this place called The Golden Trough—terrible name, I know, but it was cheap. Anyway, you'd go in thinking it was going to be great, but by plate three you're just chewing because it's there. We had this one roommate, Dave, who would always try to sneak food out in his cargo shorts. Can you believe that? Mashed potatoes in a Ziploc bag in his pocket. He actually ended up becoming an accountant, which is hilarious when you think about it. I wonder if he still wears those cargo shorts. They really went out of style, didn't they? I tried to find a good pair of regular shorts last summer and everything is so short now. It's like the 80s are back. But yeah, no, I wouldn't say I feel any pleasure when I'm eating like that now.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes usually happen when you're feeling stressed or upset about something?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really noticed if I'm stressed right before it happens. I mean, I guess I have normal stress? Like at the store, they just installed this new point-of-sale system at the registers, and corporate told us it was going to make everything so much faster, but it completely freezes if someone tries to split a payment. It's a nightmare. \n\nJust yesterday I had a line of like six people, and this woman was trying to use these expired manufacturer coupons, and the machine just completely locked up on me. People get so intense about coupons, you know? It's like a competitive sport to them. I used to watch that show, Extreme Couponing, and they'd have these giant three-ring binders full of them. I always wondered where they stored all that stuff they bought. Like, nobody needs fifty bottles of mustard. Mustard goes bad eventually, right? I'm pretty sure it separates and gets weird. I usually just buy the store brand anyway, it tastes exactly the same. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if I'm actually upset when the eating thing starts. I haven't really connected it to a specific bad mood or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Are these eating episodes ever triggered by or associated with sexual activity?\n\nPatient: No, no, definitely not. I mean, I'm not even really dating anyone right now anyway, so that hasn't been a factor at all. \n\nI actually tried getting on one of those dating apps a few months ago. Hinge, I think it was? The one where you have to answer those weird prompts like \"my most controversial opinion is...\" I never know what to put for those things. If you say something too out there, people think you're completely crazy, but if you just say \"pineapple belongs on pizza,\" it's so boring. Every other profile says that. I actually don't mind pineapple on pizza, honestly. There's this place down the street from my apartment that does a Hawaiian pizza but they add jalapeños to it, and it's surprisingly good. The sweet and spicy thing really works together. \n\nThough the last time I ordered from them, the delivery guy got completely lost and I had to walk three blocks in the rain to find his car. He was using this ancient, bulky GPS thing mounted to his dashboard instead of just using his phone. Who doesn't use their phone for maps now? It blew my mind. He had this whole setup with cords everywhere. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no, nothing to do with sex or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in sex around the time of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I haven't noticed any change in that at all. Like I said, I'm not really seeing anyone, so it's not something I think about much. \n\nIt's funny though, talking about changes in interest... my buddy Mike just suddenly lost all interest in golf. We used to go to this driving range out by the highway every other weekend. It wasn't anything fancy, just these old astroturf mats and buckets of balls that looked like they'd been chewed on by dogs. But he just stopped wanting to go. Said he was getting into woodworking instead. Woodworking! The guy lives in a third-floor apartment with carpet. I have no idea where he's supposedly building these birdhouses or whatever it is he's doing. Sawdust gets everywhere, you know? My dad used to have a table saw in the garage when I was a kid, and the dust would coat the windshield of my mom's station wagon so thick she'd have to use the wipers before backing out. It was a terrible car, that station wagon. Fake wood paneling on the sides. Very Brady Bunch. \n\nBut no, my sex drive or interest in it hasn't really changed or fluctuated around the eating stuff. It's just been pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel empty or unfulfilled inside when these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if \"empty\" is the right word. I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. I don't think I feel unfulfilled, exactly. \n\nSpeaking of feeling unfulfilled, though, my manager at the store is always talking about how unfulfilled she is by retail. She's been there for like fifteen years, and she always says she was meant to be a landscape architect. She actually brings these massive gardening catalogs into the breakroom and just circles things while eating her microwave oatmeal. The breakroom microwave is disgusting, by the way. Nobody ever covers their food, so the ceiling of it is just coated in splattered soup and whatever else. I tried to clean it once with those Clorox wipes, but it just smeared the grease around. You really need something heavy-duty for that kind of baked-on mess, like that orange citrus degreaser stuff. My uncle used to use that in his auto shop. It smelled so strong it would give you a headache if you didn't have the big bay doors open. He had this massive German Shepherd that would just sleep right in the middle of the shop floor, right where all the cars were pulling in. Dog never moved a muscle. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know about feeling empty or unfulfilled when I eat like that. I haven't really noticed feeling that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat like this as a way to deal with feeling upset or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't think so? I've never really thought of it as a way to deal with being upset or overwhelmed. I just kind of... do it. I don't know if there's a specific feeling attached to it before it happens. \n\nThough speaking of being overwhelmed, it's been completely insane at work lately. We had this massive shipment of seasonal stuff come in, and the stockroom is just a disaster zone. They have me on the register most of the time, but yesterday they made me go back there to find a specific brand of patio lights for a customer. I was climbing over boxes of inflatable pool toys for like twenty minutes. Those inflatable things smell so weird when they're fresh out of the plastic, you know? Like strong chemicals and fake vinyl. It reminds me of this water park my parents took me to when I was a kid. The whole place smelled like chlorine and hot plastic. We went on this one slide that was completely pitch black inside, and my mom lost her sunglasses halfway down. They were these huge, oversized tortoiseshell ones. Very 80s. She was so mad she made us leave early and we had to stop at a gas station to buy her a cheap replacement pair that kept sliding down her nose the whole drive home. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think I eat because I'm upset or anything like that. It doesn't feel like a coping thing, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. That's... that's exactly what brought me in, really. Some nights, usually after work or if I've had a really tense dinner or something, I'll start eating and it just feels like the decision-making part of my brain completely shuts off. Like, I can literally see my hand reaching for more food, but I can't make myself stop doing it. \n\nIt's sort of like when you're driving on the highway and you get highway hypnosis, you know? Like you just zone out and suddenly you're three exits past where you were supposed to get off. My buddy Dave did that once when we were driving out to Vegas. We were supposed to stop in Barstow to get gas and grab something to eat at this specific diner he liked, but he just totally spaced out and drove right past it. We ended up having to stop at this creepy little gas station in the middle of nowhere that only sold beef jerky and expired diet soda. The guy working the counter had this crazy glass eye that didn't move when the other one did. It was really distracting trying to pay for our stuff. Dave bought like three bags of that jerky and his jaw was sore for two days from chewing it. He always buys the teriyaki flavor, which I think is way too sweet. I'm more of a cracked black pepper guy myself. \n\nBut yeah, it's that same kind of zoned-out feeling. Once I start eating on those nights, I just can't seem to hit the brakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating episodes are triggered by feeling anxious or emotional?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I haven't noticed feeling particularly emotional or anxious right before it happens. It doesn't really feel connected to my mood, to be honest. \n\nSpeaking of being anxious, though, there was this woman at my register yesterday who was just vibrating with anxiety over her coupons. She had this massive, heavy-duty three-ring binder that was completely color-coded with these little sticky tabs. Which is crazy because we're mostly a home goods store, but she was treating it like extreme couponing at a supermarket. Anyway, she was arguing with me over this twenty-cent discount on some decorative jar candles, and the coupon had expired in like, 2019. The line behind her was getting so backed up. There was this one guy right behind her holding two massive bags of cat food, just sighing incredibly loudly every ten seconds. It was that really expensive grain-free cat food, too, the kind with the salmon on the bag. My buddy Mark used to buy that for his Maine Coon. That cat was an absolute unit, like twenty-five pounds of just pure fluff and muscle. It used to sit on top of his refrigerator and swat at anyone who walked into the kitchen. Mark actually had to put up this little wooden baby gate to keep it out of the living room when he had guests over, but the cat would just clear it in one jump without even trying. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't really think emotions or anxiety are triggering the eating. I just haven't made that connection.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy around the time of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I mean, I still like doing the stuff I usually do. Like, I haven't stopped watching movies or hanging out or anything like that. \n\nActually, speaking of movies, I finally got around to watching that new sci-fi one everyone's been talking about. The one with the time travel and the giant mechanical spiders? Honestly, the special effects were pretty good, but the plot made absolutely no sense. Like, if they could just go back and stop the spiders from being built in the first place, why did they wait until the earth was basically destroyed? It's just lazy writing. It reminds me of this script my roommate in college tried to write. He was a film major, and he was obsessed with making this neo-noir detective movie, but he wanted all the characters to be played by pugs. Just... dogs in little trench coats. He actually spent like three hundred bucks buying miniature fedoras online. He made me help him film a test scene with his neighbor's pug, Buster. We spent four hours trying to get this dog to look thoughtfully at a magnifying glass, but it just kept trying to eat the props. He had this little plastic cigar that Buster completely chewed the end off of. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I haven't noticed losing interest in things I enjoy. I still do all my normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in dating or sex might be connected to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. I don't think they're connected at all. I just haven't really been interested in dating for a while now, way before the eating stuff started. \n\nDating is just exhausting these days anyway, you know? The apps are terrible. I matched with this one woman a few months ago, and her entire profile was just pictures of her at different theme parks holding giant turkey legs. Which, I mean, is a choice. We ended up meeting for coffee, and she spent the entire forty-five minutes explaining the intricate lore of some online roleplaying game she plays. She played a level 80 elven bard or something, and apparently, there was this massive political scandal in her guild because the guild leader ran off with all their virtual gold to start a rival clan. It was honestly fascinating, but I couldn't get a word in edgewise. She even brought a little notebook to draw out the family tree of all the characters involved. The barista kept giving us these weird looks because she was getting so loud and animated about this fake betrayal. She actually knocked over her latte when she was demonstrating how her character dodged an attack. \n\nSo yeah, dating just hasn't been on my radar. I don't think it has anything to do with the eating episodes, though. It's just not something I care about right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be eating as a way to calm down or deal with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I really don't think it's about calming down. Like I was saying before, I just haven't noticed any connection between how I'm feeling and when it happens. It's not like I'm stressed and then go eat, you know? \n\nSpeaking of trying to calm down, though, my neighbor has been trying to get me into meditation. He's this super intense guy, always wearing those toe-shoes, you know the ones? The ones that look like gloves for your feet. They creep me out a little, to be honest. But anyway, he set up this whole \"zen zone\" in his backyard with a little bamboo water fountain and these massive wind chimes that sound like church bells every time there's a breeze. He invited me over last weekend to try this guided breathing thing. We were sitting on these stiff little cushions on his patio, and he had this app playing whale noises on a bluetooth speaker. But his neighbor on the other side was power-washing their deck the entire time. So it was just, like, majestic whale calls mixed with this deafening roar of a pressure washer. And he just sat there with his eyes closed, completely ignoring it, while I was getting misted with dirty deck water every time the wind blew. I ended up faking a stomach ache just so I could go back inside. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think I'm eating to deal with emotions or to calm down or anything. It just doesn't feel like that's what's going on.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don't think so. I mean, it doesn't really make me feel better if I'm having a bad day or anything. It's not really connected to my mood like that. 

Actually, speaking of being upset, I had this customer at my register yesterday who was just completely losing her mind over an expired coupon for cat food. I'm a cashier at the grocery store down the street, and this woman had a cart literally overflowing with those tiny cans of wet food. I scanned her coupon and the system rejected it, so I looked at the date, and it expired in 2019. I tried to politely tell her that, and she just went off on me. She demanded to see my manager, but Dave—my manager—was in the back trying to fix the cardboard baler. Dave is obsessed with that baler, he treats it like a vintage sports car or something. He actually printed out a label that says "The Crusher" and stuck it on the front. 

So I had to just stand there for ten minutes while this woman lectured me about how her Maine Coon has a sensitive stomach and can only eat the salmon paté. She even made me look at pictures of the cat on her phone. It looked like a small lion, honestly. Just massive. And she had dressed it in a little knitted sweater in one of the photos, which just seems cruel for a cat with that much fur.

But he just kept talking and talking, kind of like how my mom gets when she calls on Sundays. I actually don't mind pineapple on pizza, honestly. There's this place down the street from my apartment that does a Hawaiian pizza but they add jalapeños to it, and it's surprisingly good. The sweet and spicy thing really works together. \n\nThough the last time I ordered from them, the delivery guy got completely lost and I had to walk three blocks in the rain to find his car. He was using this ancient, bulky GPS thing mounted to his dashboard instead of just using his phone. Who doesn't use their phone for maps now? It blew my mind. He had this whole setup with cords everywhere. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no, nothing to do with sex or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in sex around the time of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It wasn't anything fancy, just these old astroturf mats and buckets of balls that looked like they'd been chewed on by dogs. But he just stopped wanting to go. Said he was getting into woodworking instead. Woodworking! The guy lives in a third-floor apartment with carpet. I have no idea where he's supposedly building these birdhouses or whatever it is he's doing. Sawdust gets everywhere, you know? My dad used to have a table saw in the garage when I was a kid, and the dust would coat the windshield of my mom's station wagon so thick she'd have to use the wipers before backing out. It's just been pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel empty or unfulfilled inside when these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if \"empty\" is the right word. I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. I don't think I feel unfulfilled, exactly. \n\nSpeaking of feeling unfulfilled, though, my manager at the store is always talking about how unfulfilled she is by retail. She's been there for like fifteen years, and she always says she was meant to be a landscape architect. She actually brings these massive gardening catalogs into the breakroom and just circles things while eating her microwave oatmeal. The breakroom microwave is disgusting, by the way. Nobody ever covers their food, so the ceiling of it is just coated in splattered soup and whatever else. I tried to clean it once with those Clorox wipes, but it just smeared the grease around. You really need something heavy-duty for that kind of baked-on mess, like that orange citrus degreaser stuff. My uncle used to use that in his auto shop. It smelled so strong it would give you a headache if you didn't have the big bay doors open. He had this massive German Shepherd that would just sleep right in the middle of the shop floor, right where all the cars were pulling in. Dog never moved a muscle. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know about feeling empty or unfulfilled when I eat like that. I haven't really noticed feeling that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat like this as a way to deal with feeling upset or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't think so? I've never really thought of it as a way to deal with being upset or overwhelmed. I just kind of... do it. I don't know if there's a specific feeling attached to it before it happens. \n\nThough speaking of being overwhelmed, it's been completely insane at work lately. We had this massive shipment of seasonal stuff come in, and the stockroom is just a disaster zone. They have me on the register most of the time, but yesterday they made me go back there to find a specific brand of patio lights for a customer. I was climbing over boxes of inflatable pool toys for like twenty minutes. Those inflatable things smell so weird when they're fresh out of the plastic, you know? Like strong chemicals and fake vinyl. It reminds me of this water park my parents took me to when I was a kid. The whole place smelled like chlorine and hot plastic. We went on this one slide that was completely pitch black inside, and my mom lost her sunglasses halfway down. They were these huge, oversized tortoiseshell ones. Very 80s. She was so mad she made us leave early and we had to stop at a gas station to buy her a cheap replacement pair that kept sliding down her nose the whole drive home. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think I eat because I'm upset or anything like that. It doesn't feel like a coping thing, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. That's... that's exactly what brought me in, really. Some nights, usually after work or if I've had a really tense dinner or something, I'll start eating and it just feels like the decision-making part of my brain completely shuts off. Like, I can literally see my hand reaching for more food, but I can't make myself stop doing it. \n\nIt's sort of like when you're driving on the highway and you get highway hypnosis, you know? Like you just zone out and suddenly you're three exits past where you were supposed to get off. My buddy Dave did that once when we were driving out to Vegas. We were supposed to stop in Barstow to get gas and grab something to eat at this specific diner he liked, but he just totally spaced out and drove right past it. We ended up having to stop at this creepy little gas station in the middle of nowhere that only sold beef jerky and expired diet soda. The guy working the counter had this crazy glass eye that didn't move when the other one did. It was really distracting trying to pay for our stuff. Dave bought like three bags of that jerky and his jaw was sore for two days from chewing it. He always buys the teriyaki flavor, which I think is way too sweet. I'm more of a cracked black pepper guy myself. \n\nBut yeah, it's that same kind of zoned-out feeling. Once I start eating on those nights, I just can't seem to hit the brakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating episodes are triggered by feeling anxious or emotional?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I haven't noticed feeling particularly emotional or anxious right before it happens. It doesn't really feel connected to my mood, to be honest. \n\nSpeaking of being anxious, though, there was this woman at my register yesterday who was just vibrating with anxiety over her coupons. She had this massive, heavy-duty three-ring binder that was completely color-coded with these little sticky tabs. Which is crazy because we're mostly a home goods store, but she was treating it like extreme couponing at a supermarket. Anyway, she was arguing with me over this twenty-cent discount on some decorative jar candles, and the coupon had expired in like, 2019. The line behind her was getting so backed up. There was this one guy right behind her holding two massive bags of cat food, just sighing incredibly loudly every ten seconds. It was that really expensive grain-free cat food, too, the kind with the salmon on the bag. My buddy Mark used to buy that for his Maine Coon. That cat was an absolute unit, like twenty-five pounds of just pure fluff and muscle. It used to sit on top of his refrigerator and swat at anyone who walked into the kitchen. Mark actually had to put up this little wooden baby gate to keep it out of the living room when he had guests over, but the cat would just clear it in one jump without even trying. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't really think emotions or anxiety are triggering the eating. I just haven't made that connection.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy around the time of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I mean, I still like doing the stuff I usually do. Like, I haven't stopped watching movies or hanging out or anything like that. \n\nActually, speaking of movies, I finally got around to watching that new sci-fi one everyone's been talking about. The one with the time travel and the giant mechanical spiders? Honestly, the special effects were pretty good, but the plot made absolutely no sense. Like, if they could just go back and stop the spiders from being built in the first place, why did they wait until the earth was basically destroyed? It's just lazy writing. It reminds me of this script my roommate in college tried to write. He was a film major, and he was obsessed with making this neo-noir detective movie, but he wanted all the characters to be played by pugs. Just... dogs in little trench coats. He actually spent like three hundred bucks buying miniature fedoras online. He made me help him film a test scene with his neighbor's pug, Buster. We spent four hours trying to get this dog to look thoughtfully at a magnifying glass, but it just kept trying to eat the props. He had this little plastic cigar that Buster completely chewed the end off of. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I haven't noticed losing interest in things I enjoy. I still do all my normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in dating or sex might be connected to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. I don't think they're connected at all. I just haven't really been interested in dating for a while now, way before the eating stuff started. \n\nDating is just exhausting these days anyway, you know? The apps are terrible. I matched with this one woman a few months ago, and her entire profile was just pictures of her at different theme parks holding giant turkey legs. Which, I mean, is a choice. We ended up meeting for coffee, and she spent the entire forty-five minutes explaining the intricate lore of some online roleplaying game she plays. She played a level 80 elven bard or something, and apparently, there was this massive political scandal in her guild because the guild leader ran off with all their virtual gold to start a rival clan. It was honestly fascinating, but I couldn't get a word in edgewise. She even brought a little notebook to draw out the family tree of all the characters involved. The barista kept giving us these weird looks because she was getting so loud and animated about this fake betrayal. She actually knocked over her latte when she was demonstrating how her character dodged an attack. \n\nSo yeah, dating just hasn't been on my radar. I don't think it has anything to do with the eating episodes, though. It's just not something I care about right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be eating as a way to calm down or deal with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I really don't think it's about calming down. Like I was saying before, I just haven't noticed any connection between how I'm feeling and when it happens. It's not like I'm stressed and then go eat, you know? \n\nSpeaking of trying to calm down, though, my neighbor has been trying to get me into meditation. He's this super intense guy, always wearing those toe-shoes, you know the ones? The ones that look like gloves for your feet. They creep me out a little, to be honest. But anyway, he set up this whole \"zen zone\" in his backyard with a little bamboo water fountain and these massive wind chimes that sound like church bells every time there's a breeze. He invited me over last weekend to try this guided breathing thing. We were sitting on these stiff little cushions on his patio, and he had this app playing whale noises on a bluetooth speaker. But his neighbor on the other side was power-washing their deck the entire time. So it was just, like, majestic whale calls mixed with this deafening roar of a pressure washer. And he just sat there with his eyes closed, completely ignoring it, while I was getting misted with dirty deck water every time the wind blew. I ended up faking a stomach ache just so I could go back inside. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think I'm eating to deal with emotions or to calm down or anything. It just doesn't feel like that's what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating helps you feel better when you're feeling down or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, it doesn't really make me feel better if I'm having a bad day or anything. It's not really connected to my mood like that. \n\nActually, speaking of being upset, I had this customer at my register yesterday who was just completely losing her mind over an expired coupon for cat food. I'm a cashier at the grocery store down the street, and this woman had a cart literally overflowing with those tiny cans of wet food. I scanned her coupon and the system rejected it, so I looked at the date, and it expired in 2019. I tried to politely tell her that, and she just went off on me. She demanded to see my manager, but Dave—my manager—was in the back trying to fix the cardboard baler. Dave is obsessed with that baler, he treats it like a vintage sports car or something. He actually printed out a label that says \"The Crusher\" and stuck it on the front. \n\nSo I had to just stand there for ten minutes while this woman lectured me about how her Maine Coon has a sensitive stomach and can only eat the salmon paté. She even made me look at pictures of the cat on her phone. It looked like a small lion, honestly. Just massive. And she had dressed it in a little knitted sweater in one of the photos, which just seems cruel for a cat with that much fur. \n\nBut, um, what were we talking about? Oh, right, eating when I'm down. Yeah, no, it doesn't really work like that for me. I don't notice the eating helping me feel better or anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don't know if there's a specific feeling attached to it before it happens. \n\nThough speaking of being overwhelmed, it's been completely insane at work lately. We had this massive shipment of seasonal stuff come in, and the stockroom is just a disaster zone. They have me on the register most of the time, but yesterday they made me go back there to find a specific brand of patio lights for a customer. I was climbing over boxes of inflatable pool toys for like twenty minutes. Those inflatable things smell so weird when they're fresh out of the plastic, you know? Like strong chemicals and fake vinyl. It reminds me of this water park my parents took me to when I was a kid. The whole place smelled like chlorine and hot plastic. We went on this one slide that was completely pitch black inside, and my mom lost her sunglasses halfway down. They were these huge, oversized tortoiseshell ones. Very 80s. She was so mad she made us leave early and we had to stop at a gas station to buy her a cheap replacement pair that kept sliding down her nose the whole drive home. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think I eat because I'm upset or anything like that. It doesn't feel like a coping thing, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. That's... that's exactly what brought me in, really. Some nights, usually after work or if I've had a really tense dinner or something, I'll start eating and it just feels like the decision-making part of my brain completely shuts off. Like, I can literally see my hand reaching for more food, but I can't make myself stop doing it. \n\nIt's sort of like when you're driving on the highway and you get highway hypnosis, you know? Like you just zone out and suddenly you're three exits past where you were supposed to get off. My buddy Dave did that once when we were driving out to Vegas. We were supposed to stop in Barstow to get gas and grab something to eat at this specific diner he liked, but he just totally spaced out and drove right past it. We ended up having to stop at this creepy little gas station in the middle of nowhere that only sold beef jerky and expired diet soda. The guy working the counter had this crazy glass eye that didn't move when the other one did. It was really distracting trying to pay for our stuff. Dave bought like three bags of that jerky and his jaw was sore for two days from chewing it. He always buys the teriyaki flavor, which I think is way too sweet. I'm more of a cracked black pepper guy myself. \n\nBut yeah, it's that same kind of zoned-out feeling. Once I start eating on those nights, I just can't seem to hit the brakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating episodes are triggered by feeling anxious or emotional?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I haven't noticed feeling particularly emotional or anxious right before it happens. It doesn't really feel connected to my mood, to be honest. \n\nSpeaking of being anxious, though, there was this woman at my register yesterday who was just vibrating with anxiety over her coupons. She had this massive, heavy-duty three-ring binder that was completely color-coded with these little sticky tabs. Which is crazy because we're mostly a home goods store, but she was treating it like extreme couponing at a supermarket. Anyway, she was arguing with me over this twenty-cent discount on some decorative jar candles, and the coupon had expired in like, 2019. The line behind her was getting so backed up. There was this one guy right behind her holding two massive bags of cat food, just sighing incredibly loudly every ten seconds. It was that really expensive grain-free cat food, too, the kind with the salmon on the bag. My buddy Mark used to buy that for his Maine Coon. That cat was an absolute unit, like twenty-five pounds of just pure fluff and muscle. It used to sit on top of his refrigerator and swat at anyone who walked into the kitchen. Mark actually had to put up this little wooden baby gate to keep it out of the living room when he had guests over, but the cat would just clear it in one jump without even trying. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't really think emotions or anxiety are triggering the eating. I just haven't made that connection.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy around the time of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I mean, I still like doing the stuff I usually do. Like, I haven't stopped watching movies or hanging out or anything like that. \n\nActually, speaking of movies, I finally got around to watching that new sci-fi one everyone's been talking about. The one with the time travel and the giant mechanical spiders? Honestly, the special effects were pretty good, but the plot made absolutely no sense. Like, if they could just go back and stop the spiders from being built in the first place, why did they wait until the earth was basically destroyed? It's just lazy writing. It reminds me of this script my roommate in college tried to write. He was a film major, and he was obsessed with making this neo-noir detective movie, but he wanted all the characters to be played by pugs. Just... dogs in little trench coats. He actually spent like three hundred bucks buying miniature fedoras online. He made me help him film a test scene with his neighbor's pug, Buster. We spent four hours trying to get this dog to look thoughtfully at a magnifying glass, but it just kept trying to eat the props. He had this little plastic cigar that Buster completely chewed the end off of. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I haven't noticed losing interest in things I enjoy. I still do all my normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in dating or sex might be connected to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. I don't think they're connected at all. I just haven't really been interested in dating for a while now, way before the eating stuff started. \n\nDating is just exhausting these days anyway, you know? The apps are terrible. I matched with this one woman a few months ago, and her entire profile was just pictures of her at different theme parks holding giant turkey legs. Which, I mean, is a choice. We ended up meeting for coffee, and she spent the entire forty-five minutes explaining the intricate lore of some online roleplaying game she plays. She played a level 80 elven bard or something, and apparently, there was this massive political scandal in her guild because the guild leader ran off with all their virtual gold to start a rival clan. It was honestly fascinating, but I couldn't get a word in edgewise. She even brought a little notebook to draw out the family tree of all the characters involved. The barista kept giving us these weird looks because she was getting so loud and animated about this fake betrayal. She actually knocked over her latte when she was demonstrating how her character dodged an attack. \n\nSo yeah, dating just hasn't been on my radar. I don't think it has anything to do with the eating episodes, though. It's just not something I care about right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be eating as a way to calm down or deal with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I really don't think it's about calming down. Like I was saying before, I just haven't noticed any connection between how I'm feeling and when it happens. It's not like I'm stressed and then go eat, you know? \n\nSpeaking of trying to calm down, though, my neighbor has been trying to get me into meditation. He's this super intense guy, always wearing those toe-shoes, you know the ones? The ones that look like gloves for your feet. They creep me out a little, to be honest. But anyway, he set up this whole \"zen zone\" in his backyard with a little bamboo water fountain and these massive wind chimes that sound like church bells every time there's a breeze. He invited me over last weekend to try this guided breathing thing. We were sitting on these stiff little cushions on his patio, and he had this app playing whale noises on a bluetooth speaker. But his neighbor on the other side was power-washing their deck the entire time. So it was just, like, majestic whale calls mixed with this deafening roar of a pressure washer. And he just sat there with his eyes closed, completely ignoring it, while I was getting misted with dirty deck water every time the wind blew. I ended up faking a stomach ache just so I could go back inside. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think I'm eating to deal with emotions or to calm down or anything. It just doesn't feel like that's what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating helps you feel better when you're feeling down or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, it doesn't really make me feel better if I'm having a bad day or anything. It's not really connected to my mood like that. \n\nActually, speaking of being upset, I had this customer at my register yesterday who was just completely losing her mind over an expired coupon for cat food. I'm a cashier at the grocery store down the street, and this woman had a cart literally overflowing with those tiny cans of wet food. I scanned her coupon and the system rejected it, so I looked at the date, and it expired in 2019. I tried to politely tell her that, and she just went off on me. She demanded to see my manager, but Dave—my manager—was in the back trying to fix the cardboard baler. Dave is obsessed with that baler, he treats it like a vintage sports car or something. He actually printed out a label that says \"The Crusher\" and stuck it on the front. \n\nSo I had to just stand there for ten minutes while this woman lectured me about how her Maine Coon has a sensitive stomach and can only eat the salmon paté. She even made me look at pictures of the cat on her phone. It looked like a small lion, honestly. Just massive. And she had dressed it in a little knitted sweater in one of the photos, which just seems cruel for a cat with that much fur. \n\nBut, um, what were we talking about? Oh, right, eating when I'm down. Yeah, no, it doesn't really work like that for me. I don't notice the eating helping me feel better or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating in response to any emotional feelings, such as sadness or anxiety, when these episodes happen?\n\nPatient: I know we just kind of touched on this, but no, I really don't feel like it's from being sad or anxious. I just haven't noticed any emotional trigger like that. \n\nHonestly, if you want to see real anxiety, you should watch people use the self-checkout machines at my store. It is wild. It's like the second they step up to the screen, they completely forget how to read. This one guy yesterday was trying to buy a bag of Honeycrisp apples, but he kept aggressively tapping the button for bananas. And the machine is just blaring, \"Please place your item in the bagging area,\" over and over, and he's just staring at the scanner like it's an alien spaceship. He was sweating profusely, like, dripping sweat onto the glass. I had to walk over with my little barcode card and override the system, and he looked at me like I had just defused a bomb. \n\nHe actually tried to tip me a dollar, which we aren't even allowed to accept because corporate considers it a \"conflict of interest.\" They made us watch this whole two-hour training video about bribery last month, which is hilarious because who is bribing a grocery store cashier? Like, what kind of underground produce smuggling ring do they think we're running out of lane four? The video had these terrible actors in oversized suits sliding envelopes of cash across a breakroom table. \n\nBut, uh, wait, sorry. What was the question again? Oh, right, my emotions. Yeah, no, I don't really notice any sadness or anxiety causing the eating. It just doesn't feel connected to my mood.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Sex? Um, no, I haven't really noticed any change there, or any connection to the eating stuff. I mean, things are pretty much the same as they've always been in that department.

I have no idea where he's supposedly building these birdhouses or whatever it is he's doing. Sawdust gets everywhere, you know? My dad used to have a table saw in the garage when I was a kid, and the dust would coat the windshield of my mom's station wagon so thick she'd have to use the wipers before backing out. It was a terrible car, that station wagon. Fake wood paneling on the sides. Very Brady Bunch. \n\nBut no, my sex drive or interest in it hasn't really changed or fluctuated around the eating stuff. It's just been pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel empty or unfulfilled inside when these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if \"empty\" is the right word. I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. I don't think I feel unfulfilled, exactly. \n\nSpeaking of feeling unfulfilled, though, my manager at the store is always talking about how unfulfilled she is by retail. She's been there for like fifteen years, and she always says she was meant to be a landscape architect. She actually brings these massive gardening catalogs into the breakroom and just circles things while eating her microwave oatmeal. The breakroom microwave is disgusting, by the way. Nobody ever covers their food, so the ceiling of it is just coated in splattered soup and whatever else. I tried to clean it once with those Clorox wipes, but it just smeared the grease around. You really need something heavy-duty for that kind of baked-on mess, like that orange citrus degreaser stuff. My uncle used to use that in his auto shop. It smelled so strong it would give you a headache if you didn't have the big bay doors open. He had this massive German Shepherd that would just sleep right in the middle of the shop floor, right where all the cars were pulling in. Dog never moved a muscle. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know about feeling empty or unfulfilled when I eat like that. I haven't really noticed feeling that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat like this as a way to deal with feeling upset or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't think so? I've never really thought of it as a way to deal with being upset or overwhelmed. I just kind of... do it. I don't know if there's a specific feeling attached to it before it happens. \n\nThough speaking of being overwhelmed, it's been completely insane at work lately. We had this massive shipment of seasonal stuff come in, and the stockroom is just a disaster zone. They have me on the register most of the time, but yesterday they made me go back there to find a specific brand of patio lights for a customer. I was climbing over boxes of inflatable pool toys for like twenty minutes. Those inflatable things smell so weird when they're fresh out of the plastic, you know? Like strong chemicals and fake vinyl. It reminds me of this water park my parents took me to when I was a kid. The whole place smelled like chlorine and hot plastic. We went on this one slide that was completely pitch black inside, and my mom lost her sunglasses halfway down. They were these huge, oversized tortoiseshell ones. Very 80s. She was so mad she made us leave early and we had to stop at a gas station to buy her a cheap replacement pair that kept sliding down her nose the whole drive home. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think I eat because I'm upset or anything like that. It doesn't feel like a coping thing, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. That's... that's exactly what brought me in, really. Some nights, usually after work or if I've had a really tense dinner or something, I'll start eating and it just feels like the decision-making part of my brain completely shuts off. Like, I can literally see my hand reaching for more food, but I can't make myself stop doing it. \n\nIt's sort of like when you're driving on the highway and you get highway hypnosis, you know? Like you just zone out and suddenly you're three exits past where you were supposed to get off. My buddy Dave did that once when we were driving out to Vegas. We were supposed to stop in Barstow to get gas and grab something to eat at this specific diner he liked, but he just totally spaced out and drove right past it. We ended up having to stop at this creepy little gas station in the middle of nowhere that only sold beef jerky and expired diet soda. The guy working the counter had this crazy glass eye that didn't move when the other one did. It was really distracting trying to pay for our stuff. Dave bought like three bags of that jerky and his jaw was sore for two days from chewing it. He always buys the teriyaki flavor, which I think is way too sweet. I'm more of a cracked black pepper guy myself. \n\nBut yeah, it's that same kind of zoned-out feeling. Once I start eating on those nights, I just can't seem to hit the brakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating episodes are triggered by feeling anxious or emotional?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I haven't noticed feeling particularly emotional or anxious right before it happens. It doesn't really feel connected to my mood, to be honest. \n\nSpeaking of being anxious, though, there was this woman at my register yesterday who was just vibrating with anxiety over her coupons. She had this massive, heavy-duty three-ring binder that was completely color-coded with these little sticky tabs. Which is crazy because we're mostly a home goods store, but she was treating it like extreme couponing at a supermarket. Anyway, she was arguing with me over this twenty-cent discount on some decorative jar candles, and the coupon had expired in like, 2019. The line behind her was getting so backed up. There was this one guy right behind her holding two massive bags of cat food, just sighing incredibly loudly every ten seconds. It was that really expensive grain-free cat food, too, the kind with the salmon on the bag. My buddy Mark used to buy that for his Maine Coon. That cat was an absolute unit, like twenty-five pounds of just pure fluff and muscle. It used to sit on top of his refrigerator and swat at anyone who walked into the kitchen. Mark actually had to put up this little wooden baby gate to keep it out of the living room when he had guests over, but the cat would just clear it in one jump without even trying. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't really think emotions or anxiety are triggering the eating. I just haven't made that connection.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy around the time of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I mean, I still like doing the stuff I usually do. Like, I haven't stopped watching movies or hanging out or anything like that. \n\nActually, speaking of movies, I finally got around to watching that new sci-fi one everyone's been talking about. The one with the time travel and the giant mechanical spiders? Honestly, the special effects were pretty good, but the plot made absolutely no sense. Like, if they could just go back and stop the spiders from being built in the first place, why did they wait until the earth was basically destroyed? It's just lazy writing. It reminds me of this script my roommate in college tried to write. He was a film major, and he was obsessed with making this neo-noir detective movie, but he wanted all the characters to be played by pugs. Just... dogs in little trench coats. He actually spent like three hundred bucks buying miniature fedoras online. He made me help him film a test scene with his neighbor's pug, Buster. We spent four hours trying to get this dog to look thoughtfully at a magnifying glass, but it just kept trying to eat the props. He had this little plastic cigar that Buster completely chewed the end off of. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I haven't noticed losing interest in things I enjoy. I still do all my normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in dating or sex might be connected to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. I don't think they're connected at all. I just haven't really been interested in dating for a while now, way before the eating stuff started. \n\nDating is just exhausting these days anyway, you know? The apps are terrible. I matched with this one woman a few months ago, and her entire profile was just pictures of her at different theme parks holding giant turkey legs. Which, I mean, is a choice. We ended up meeting for coffee, and she spent the entire forty-five minutes explaining the intricate lore of some online roleplaying game she plays. She played a level 80 elven bard or something, and apparently, there was this massive political scandal in her guild because the guild leader ran off with all their virtual gold to start a rival clan. It was honestly fascinating, but I couldn't get a word in edgewise. She even brought a little notebook to draw out the family tree of all the characters involved. The barista kept giving us these weird looks because she was getting so loud and animated about this fake betrayal. She actually knocked over her latte when she was demonstrating how her character dodged an attack. \n\nSo yeah, dating just hasn't been on my radar. I don't think it has anything to do with the eating episodes, though. It's just not something I care about right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be eating as a way to calm down or deal with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I really don't think it's about calming down. Like I was saying before, I just haven't noticed any connection between how I'm feeling and when it happens. It's not like I'm stressed and then go eat, you know? \n\nSpeaking of trying to calm down, though, my neighbor has been trying to get me into meditation. He's this super intense guy, always wearing those toe-shoes, you know the ones? The ones that look like gloves for your feet. They creep me out a little, to be honest. But anyway, he set up this whole \"zen zone\" in his backyard with a little bamboo water fountain and these massive wind chimes that sound like church bells every time there's a breeze. He invited me over last weekend to try this guided breathing thing. We were sitting on these stiff little cushions on his patio, and he had this app playing whale noises on a bluetooth speaker. But his neighbor on the other side was power-washing their deck the entire time. So it was just, like, majestic whale calls mixed with this deafening roar of a pressure washer. And he just sat there with his eyes closed, completely ignoring it, while I was getting misted with dirty deck water every time the wind blew. I ended up faking a stomach ache just so I could go back inside. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think I'm eating to deal with emotions or to calm down or anything. It just doesn't feel like that's what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating helps you feel better when you're feeling down or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, it doesn't really make me feel better if I'm having a bad day or anything. It's not really connected to my mood like that. \n\nActually, speaking of being upset, I had this customer at my register yesterday who was just completely losing her mind over an expired coupon for cat food. I'm a cashier at the grocery store down the street, and this woman had a cart literally overflowing with those tiny cans of wet food. I scanned her coupon and the system rejected it, so I looked at the date, and it expired in 2019. I tried to politely tell her that, and she just went off on me. She demanded to see my manager, but Dave—my manager—was in the back trying to fix the cardboard baler. Dave is obsessed with that baler, he treats it like a vintage sports car or something. He actually printed out a label that says \"The Crusher\" and stuck it on the front. \n\nSo I had to just stand there for ten minutes while this woman lectured me about how her Maine Coon has a sensitive stomach and can only eat the salmon paté. She even made me look at pictures of the cat on her phone. It looked like a small lion, honestly. Just massive. And she had dressed it in a little knitted sweater in one of the photos, which just seems cruel for a cat with that much fur. \n\nBut, um, what were we talking about? Oh, right, eating when I'm down. Yeah, no, it doesn't really work like that for me. I don't notice the eating helping me feel better or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating in response to any emotional feelings, such as sadness or anxiety, when these episodes happen?\n\nPatient: I know we just kind of touched on this, but no, I really don't feel like it's from being sad or anxious. I just haven't noticed any emotional trigger like that. \n\nHonestly, if you want to see real anxiety, you should watch people use the self-checkout machines at my store. It is wild. And the machine is just blaring, \"Please place your item in the bagging area,\" over and over, and he's just staring at the scanner like it's an alien spaceship. He was sweating profusely, like, dripping sweat onto the glass. Like, what kind of underground produce smuggling ring do they think we're running out of lane four? The video had these terrible actors in oversized suits sliding envelopes of cash across a breakroom table. \n\nBut, uh, wait, sorry. What was the question again? Oh, right, my emotions. She didn't even seem to notice that her dog was actively dissolving my pants. \n\nSo, yeah, dating is exhausting. Yeah, that's... that's actually exactly what brought me in. It's like, some nights after work, or maybe if I've had a really tense dinner, I'll start eating and it just feels like the decision-making part of my brain completely shuts off. I can literally see myself reaching for more food, but I just can't make myself stop. It's the weirdest feeling. 

Speaking of tense dinners, though, I went to my sister's house for dinner on Sunday, and her kids are just... they're menaces. She has these twin boys, they're seven, and they have decided that their new favorite game is "Floor is Lava," but like, an extreme version where they hurl themselves off the furniture like stuntmen. One of them, Liam, launched himself off the back of the sofa and landed squarely in the middle of the coffee table. It didn't break, miraculously, but he knocked over a full pitcher of iced tea. It went everywhere. And my sister didn't even blink! She just kept talking about her new air fryer like she was filming an infomercial. She was telling me how you can make a hard-boiled egg in it, which doesn't even make sense because it's not boiling if there's no water, right? It's just a hot egg. I tried to explain the physics of that to her, but she just waved me off and told me I was being closed-minded about modern appliances. 

And meanwhile, I'm sitting there with iced tea seeping into my socks, watching her kids try to scale the curtains. 

But, um, anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, right, controlling the eating. Yeah, no, I definitely feel like I can't stop once I start. It's like I'm on autopilot or something.

You wouldn't believe the customers we get in there. Just this past Tuesday, this guy started screaming at me over the price of organic avocados. Like I'm the one who sets the prices! I have a bachelor's degree, you know, I didn't think I'd be 42 years old and getting yelled at over produce. My manager didn't even come out of the back room to help, which is just typical for him. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I get home and I start eating, and it's like... I can literally see myself reaching for more food, but I just can’t make myself stop. It's really frustrating. Kinda like how the traffic has been on my commute home lately, honestly. Just bumper to bumper on the highway, and you're just stuck there watching the cars, unable to do anything about it. Have they been doing construction out by your place too? Because it took me forever just to get to this clinic today.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control while you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. That's exactly it. Like I was saying, it's like that decision-making part of my brain just completely powers down. I'll be standing there in the kitchen, and it's like I'm watching myself from the outside. I can see my hand reaching for another sleeve of cookies or whatever, and I just can't make myself stop. It's so weird. \n\nActually, it reminds me of this documentary I watched last weekend about deep-sea divers. Did you know that when they go down too far, they get this thing called nitrogen narcosis? It's like they're drunk underwater, and they know they're doing something dangerous, but their brain just won't let them correct it. Fascinating stuff, really. I always wanted to learn how to scuba dive, but living here, the nearest decent water is, what, three hours away? Plus, with my schedule at the store, getting a whole weekend off is basically impossible. My manager, Greg, he always schedules me for the Saturday morning shifts. Every single time. But yeah, no, I definitely don't feel in control when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I feel awful afterward. It's just this heavy, sinking feeling, you know? Like, why did I just do that? And then I'm standing there looking at all the empty wrappers and containers, and I just feel so disgusted with myself. \n\nIt actually feels a lot like how I felt after I bought my car. I went to this dealership out on Route 9, and the salesman—this guy in a really loud plaid suit, I swear he looked like he stepped out of the 1970s—he totally fast-talked me into getting the extended warranty and the undercoating and all this stuff I didn't need. I drove off the lot and just felt this immediate wave of regret. Like, I knew better, you know? I have a degree. I shouldn't be falling for those kinds of tricks. But he just kept talking and talking, kind of like how my mom gets when she calls on Sundays. She'll just go on for an hour about her neighbor's dog digging up her petunias, and I can't get a word in edgewise. Have you ever tried growing petunias? I hear they're really finicky. But yeah, the guilt is definitely there. It's really overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: Do you enjoy the food or feel any pleasure while you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if I enjoy it. I mean, maybe for the first bite or two? But then it just turns into this frantic thing, and I'm not even tasting it anymore. It's just... I don't know, it's not about pleasure. \n\nIt's actually a lot like when I used to go to these all-you-can-eat buffets with my roommate back in college. We'd go to this place called The Golden Trough—terrible name, I know, but it was cheap. Anyway, you'd go in thinking it was going to be great, but by plate three you're just chewing because it's there. We had this one roommate, Dave, who would always try to sneak food out in his cargo shorts. Can you believe that? Mashed potatoes in a Ziploc bag in his pocket. He actually ended up becoming an accountant, which is hilarious when you think about it. I wonder if he still wears those cargo shorts. They really went out of style, didn't they? I tried to find a good pair of regular shorts last summer and everything is so short now. It's like the 80s are back. But yeah, no, I wouldn't say I feel any pleasure when I'm eating like that now.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes usually happen when you're feeling stressed or upset about something?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really noticed if I'm stressed right before it happens. I mean, I guess I have normal stress? Like at the store, they just installed this new point-of-sale system at the registers, and corporate told us it was going to make everything so much faster, but it completely freezes if someone tries to split a payment. It's a nightmare. \n\nJust yesterday I had a line of like six people, and this woman was trying to use these expired manufacturer coupons, and the machine just completely locked up on me. People get so intense about coupons, you know? It's like a competitive sport to them. I used to watch that show, Extreme Couponing, and they'd have these giant three-ring binders full of them. I always wondered where they stored all that stuff they bought. Like, nobody needs fifty bottles of mustard. Mustard goes bad eventually, right? I'm pretty sure it separates and gets weird. I usually just buy the store brand anyway, it tastes exactly the same. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if I'm actually upset when the eating thing starts. I haven't really connected it to a specific bad mood or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Are these eating episodes ever triggered by or associated with sexual activity?\n\nPatient: No, no, definitely not. The sweet and spicy thing really works together. \n\nThough the last time I ordered from them, the delivery guy got completely lost and I had to walk three blocks in the rain to find his car. He had this whole setup with cords everywhere. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no, nothing to do with sex or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in sex around the time of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I haven't noticed any change in that at all. Like, I can literally see my hand reaching for more food, but I can't make myself stop doing it. \n\nIt's sort of like when you're driving on the highway and you get highway hypnosis, you know? Like you just zone out and suddenly you're three exits past where you were supposed to get off. My buddy Dave did that once when we were driving out to Vegas. We ended up having to stop at this creepy little gas station in the middle of nowhere that only sold beef jerky and expired diet soda. Like I said, I'm not really seeing anyone, so it's not something I think about much. \n\nIt's funny though, talking about changes in interest... my buddy Mike just suddenly lost all interest in golf. We used to go to this driving range out by the highway every other weekend. It wasn't anything fancy, just these old astroturf mats and buckets of balls that looked like they'd been chewed on by dogs. But he just stopped wanting to go. Said he was getting into woodworking instead. Woodworking! The guy lives in a third-floor apartment with carpet. I have no idea where he's supposedly building these birdhouses or whatever it is he's doing. Sawdust gets everywhere, you know? My dad used to have a table saw in the garage when I was a kid, and the dust would coat the windshield of my mom's station wagon so thick she'd have to use the wipers before backing out. It was a terrible car, that station wagon. Fake wood paneling on the sides. Very Brady Bunch. \n\nBut no, my sex drive or interest in it hasn't really changed or fluctuated around the eating stuff. It's just been pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel empty or unfulfilled inside when these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if \"empty\" is the right word. I haven't really thought about it like that, to be honest. I don't think I feel unfulfilled, exactly. \n\nSpeaking of feeling unfulfilled, though, my manager at the store is always talking about how unfulfilled she is by retail. She's been there for like fifteen years, and she always says she was meant to be a landscape architect. She actually brings these massive gardening catalogs into the breakroom and just circles things while eating her microwave oatmeal. The breakroom microwave is disgusting, by the way. Nobody ever covers their food, so the ceiling of it is just coated in splattered soup and whatever else. I tried to clean it once with those Clorox wipes, but it just smeared the grease around. You really need something heavy-duty for that kind of baked-on mess, like that orange citrus degreaser stuff. My uncle used to use that in his auto shop. It smelled so strong it would give you a headache if you didn't have the big bay doors open. He had this massive German Shepherd that would just sleep right in the middle of the shop floor, right where all the cars were pulling in. Dog never moved a muscle. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know about feeling empty or unfulfilled when I eat like that. I haven't really noticed feeling that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat like this as a way to deal with feeling upset or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't think so? I've never really thought of it as a way to deal with being upset or overwhelmed. I just kind of... do it. I don't know if there's a specific feeling attached to it before it happens. \n\nThough speaking of being overwhelmed, it's been completely insane at work lately. We had this massive shipment of seasonal stuff come in, and the stockroom is just a disaster zone. They have me on the register most of the time, but yesterday they made me go back there to find a specific brand of patio lights for a customer. I was climbing over boxes of inflatable pool toys for like twenty minutes. Those inflatable things smell so weird when they're fresh out of the plastic, you know? Like strong chemicals and fake vinyl. It reminds me of this water park my parents took me to when I was a kid. The whole place smelled like chlorine and hot plastic. We went on this one slide that was completely pitch black inside, and my mom lost her sunglasses halfway down. They were these huge, oversized tortoiseshell ones. Very 80s. She was so mad she made us leave early and we had to stop at a gas station to buy her a cheap replacement pair that kept sliding down her nose the whole drive home. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think I eat because I'm upset or anything like that. It doesn't feel like a coping thing, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. That's... that's exactly what brought me in, really. Some nights, usually after work or if I've had a really tense dinner or something, I'll start eating and it just feels like the decision-making part of my brain completely shuts off. Like, I can literally see my hand reaching for more food, but I can't make myself stop doing it. \n\nIt's sort of like when you're driving on the highway and you get highway hypnosis, you know? Like you just zone out and suddenly you're three exits past where you were supposed to get off. My buddy Dave did that once when we were driving out to Vegas. We were supposed to stop in Barstow to get gas and grab something to eat at this specific diner he liked, but he just totally spaced out and drove right past it. We ended up having to stop at this creepy little gas station in the middle of nowhere that only sold beef jerky and expired diet soda. The guy working the counter had this crazy glass eye that didn't move when the other one did. It was really distracting trying to pay for our stuff. Dave bought like three bags of that jerky and his jaw was sore for two days from chewing it. He always buys the teriyaki flavor, which I think is way too sweet. I'm more of a cracked black pepper guy myself. \n\nBut yeah, it's that same kind of zoned-out feeling. Once I start eating on those nights, I just can't seem to hit the brakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating episodes are triggered by feeling anxious or emotional?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I haven't noticed feeling particularly emotional or anxious right before it happens. It doesn't really feel connected to my mood, to be honest. \n\nSpeaking of being anxious, though, there was this woman at my register yesterday who was just vibrating with anxiety over her coupons. She had this massive, heavy-duty three-ring binder that was completely color-coded with these little sticky tabs. Which is crazy because we're mostly a home goods store, but she was treating it like extreme couponing at a supermarket. Anyway, she was arguing with me over this twenty-cent discount on some decorative jar candles, and the coupon had expired in like, 2019. The line behind her was getting so backed up. There was this one guy right behind her holding two massive bags of cat food, just sighing incredibly loudly every ten seconds. It was that really expensive grain-free cat food, too, the kind with the salmon on the bag. My buddy Mark used to buy that for his Maine Coon. That cat was an absolute unit, like twenty-five pounds of just pure fluff and muscle. It used to sit on top of his refrigerator and swat at anyone who walked into the kitchen. Mark actually had to put up this little wooden baby gate to keep it out of the living room when he had guests over, but the cat would just clear it in one jump without even trying. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't really think emotions or anxiety are triggering the eating. I just haven't made that connection.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy around the time of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I mean, I still like doing the stuff I usually do. Like, I haven't stopped watching movies or hanging out or anything like that. \n\nActually, speaking of movies, I finally got around to watching that new sci-fi one everyone's been talking about. The one with the time travel and the giant mechanical spiders? Honestly, the special effects were pretty good, but the plot made absolutely no sense. Like, if they could just go back and stop the spiders from being built in the first place, why did they wait until the earth was basically destroyed? It's just lazy writing. It reminds me of this script my roommate in college tried to write. He was a film major, and he was obsessed with making this neo-noir detective movie, but he wanted all the characters to be played by pugs. Just... dogs in little trench coats. He actually spent like three hundred bucks buying miniature fedoras online. He made me help him film a test scene with his neighbor's pug, Buster. We spent four hours trying to get this dog to look thoughtfully at a magnifying glass, but it just kept trying to eat the props. He had this little plastic cigar that Buster completely chewed the end off of. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I haven't noticed losing interest in things I enjoy. I still do all my normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in dating or sex might be connected to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. I don't think they're connected at all. I just haven't really been interested in dating for a while now, way before the eating stuff started. \n\nDating is just exhausting these days anyway, you know? The apps are terrible. I matched with this one woman a few months ago, and her entire profile was just pictures of her at different theme parks holding giant turkey legs. Which, I mean, is a choice. We ended up meeting for coffee, and she spent the entire forty-five minutes explaining the intricate lore of some online roleplaying game she plays. She played a level 80 elven bard or something, and apparently, there was this massive political scandal in her guild because the guild leader ran off with all their virtual gold to start a rival clan. It was honestly fascinating, but I couldn't get a word in edgewise. She even brought a little notebook to draw out the family tree of all the characters involved. The barista kept giving us these weird looks because she was getting so loud and animated about this fake betrayal. She actually knocked over her latte when she was demonstrating how her character dodged an attack. \n\nSo yeah, dating just hasn't been on my radar. I don't think it has anything to do with the eating episodes, though. It's just not something I care about right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be eating as a way to calm down or deal with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I really don't think it's about calming down. Like I was saying before, I just haven't noticed any connection between how I'm feeling and when it happens. Yeah, we talked about this a little bit already, but yeah. That's actually the main reason I made this appointment. It's like... some nights, usually after a long shift at the store or if I've had one of those tense dinners we talked about, I'll start eating and it's like the decision-making part of my brain just completely shuts off. I can literally watch my hand reaching for more food, but I just can't make myself stop. It's so frustrating. 

Speaking of frustrating, you know what happened at the store yesterday? The barcode scanner at my register just decided to give up on life. Right in the middle of the evening rush. And of course, it was when I had this customer who was buying, I kid you not, thirty cans of cat food. All different flavors. And because the scanner died, I had to type in the little numbers under the barcode by hand for every single one. The guy behind her in line was huffing and puffing like a steam engine, looking at his watch every five seconds. I wanted to tell him, "Look, buddy, I didn't break the scanner on purpose," but you know, customer service voice. You just have to smile and apologize. And then the cat food lady started arguing with me about a coupon that expired in 2019. She insisted that because the store was closed for a remodel back then for two weeks, we should honor it now. The logic was baffling. I had to call the manager over, and he just gave it to her anyway to make her leave, which always makes me look like the bad guy. 

But, um, anyway, yeah. The eating thing. It's exactly like that. I just feel like I have zero control once I get started. Like I'm completely on autopilot and I can't snap out of it.
