Some nights, usually after work or if I've had a really tense dinner or something, I'll start eating and it just feels like the decision-making part of my brain completely shuts off. Like, I can literally see my hand reaching for more food, but I can't make myself stop doing it. \n\nIt's sort of like when you're driving on the highway and you get highway hypnosis, you know? Like you just zone out and suddenly you're three exits past where you were supposed to get off. My buddy Dave did that once when we were driving out to Vegas. We were supposed to stop in Barstow to get gas and grab something to eat at this specific diner he liked, but he just totally spaced out and drove right past it. We ended up having to stop at this creepy little gas station in the middle of nowhere that only sold beef jerky and expired diet soda. The guy working the counter had this crazy glass eye that didn't move when the other one did. It was really distracting trying to pay for our stuff. Dave bought like three bags of that jerky and his jaw was sore for two days from chewing it. He always buys the teriyaki flavor, which I think is way too sweet. I'm more of a cracked black pepper guy myself. \n\nBut yeah, it's that same kind of zoned-out feeling. Once I start eating on those nights, I just can't seem to hit the brakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating episodes are triggered by feeling anxious or emotional?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I haven't noticed feeling particularly emotional or anxious right before it happens. It doesn't really feel connected to my mood, to be honest. \n\nSpeaking of being anxious, though, there was this woman at my register yesterday who was just vibrating with anxiety over her coupons. She had this massive, heavy-duty three-ring binder that was completely color-coded with these little sticky tabs. Which is crazy because we're mostly a home goods store, but she was treating it like extreme couponing at a supermarket. Anyway, she was arguing with me over this twenty-cent discount on some decorative jar candles, and the coupon had expired in like, 2019. The line behind her was getting so backed up. There was this one guy right behind her holding two massive bags of cat food, just sighing incredibly loudly every ten seconds. It was that really expensive grain-free cat food, too, the kind with the salmon on the bag. My buddy Mark used to buy that for his Maine Coon. That cat was an absolute unit, like twenty-five pounds of just pure fluff and muscle. It used to sit on top of his refrigerator and swat at anyone who walked into the kitchen. Mark actually had to put up this little wooden baby gate to keep it out of the living room when he had guests over, but the cat would just clear it in one jump without even trying. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't really think emotions or anxiety are triggering the eating. I just haven't made that connection.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy around the time of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I mean, I still like doing the stuff I usually do. Like, I haven't stopped watching movies or hanging out or anything like that. \n\nActually, speaking of movies, I finally got around to watching that new sci-fi one everyone's been talking about. The one with the time travel and the giant mechanical spiders? Honestly, the special effects were pretty good, but the plot made absolutely no sense. Like, if they could just go back and stop the spiders from being built in the first place, why did they wait until the earth was basically destroyed? It's just lazy writing. It reminds me of this script my roommate in college tried to write. He was a film major, and he was obsessed with making this neo-noir detective movie, but he wanted all the characters to be played by pugs. Just... dogs in little trench coats. He actually spent like three hundred bucks buying miniature fedoras online. He made me help him film a test scene with his neighbor's pug, Buster. We spent four hours trying to get this dog to look thoughtfully at a magnifying glass, but it just kept trying to eat the props. He had this little plastic cigar that Buster completely chewed the end off of. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I haven't noticed losing interest in things I enjoy. I still do all my normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in dating or sex might be connected to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, not really. I don't think they're connected at all. I just haven't really been interested in dating for a while now, way before the eating stuff started. \n\nDating is just exhausting these days anyway, you know? The apps are terrible. I matched with this one woman a few months ago, and her entire profile was just pictures of her at different theme parks holding giant turkey legs. Which, I mean, is a choice. We ended up meeting for coffee, and she spent the entire forty-five minutes explaining the intricate lore of some online roleplaying game she plays. She played a level 80 elven bard or something, and apparently, there was this massive political scandal in her guild because the guild leader ran off with all their virtual gold to start a rival clan. It was honestly fascinating, but I couldn't get a word in edgewise. She even brought a little notebook to draw out the family tree of all the characters involved. The barista kept giving us these weird looks because she was getting so loud and animated about this fake betrayal. She actually knocked over her latte when she was demonstrating how her character dodged an attack. \n\nSo yeah, dating just hasn't been on my radar. I don't think it has anything to do with the eating episodes, though. It's just not something I care about right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be eating as a way to calm down or deal with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I really don't think it's about calming down. Like I was saying before, I just haven't noticed any connection between how I'm feeling and when it happens. It's not like I'm stressed and then go eat, you know? \n\nSpeaking of trying to calm down, though, my neighbor has been trying to get me into meditation. He's this super intense guy, always wearing those toe-shoes, you know the ones? The ones that look like gloves for your feet. They creep me out a little, to be honest. But anyway, he set up this whole \"zen zone\" in his backyard with a little bamboo water fountain and these massive wind chimes that sound like church bells every time there's a breeze. He invited me over last weekend to try this guided breathing thing. We were sitting on these stiff little cushions on his patio, and he had this app playing whale noises on a bluetooth speaker. But his neighbor on the other side was power-washing their deck the entire time. So it was just, like, majestic whale calls mixed with this deafening roar of a pressure washer. And he just sat there with his eyes closed, completely ignoring it, while I was getting misted with dirty deck water every time the wind blew. I ended up faking a stomach ache just so I could go back inside. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think I'm eating to deal with emotions or to calm down or anything. It just doesn't feel like that's what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating helps you feel better when you're feeling down or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, it doesn't really make me feel better if I'm having a bad day or anything. It's not really connected to my mood like that. \n\nActually, speaking of being upset, I had this customer at my register yesterday who was just completely losing her mind over an expired coupon for cat food. I'm a cashier at the grocery store down the street, and this woman had a cart literally overflowing with those tiny cans of wet food. I scanned her coupon and the system rejected it, so I looked at the date, and it expired in 2019. I tried to politely tell her that, and she just went off on me. She demanded to see my manager, but Dave—my manager—was in the back trying to fix the cardboard baler. Dave is obsessed with that baler, he treats it like a vintage sports car or something. He actually printed out a label that says \"The Crusher\" and stuck it on the front. \n\nSo I had to just stand there for ten minutes while this woman lectured me about how her Maine Coon has a sensitive stomach and can only eat the salmon paté. She even made me look at pictures of the cat on her phone. It looked like a small lion, honestly. Just massive. And she had dressed it in a little knitted sweater in one of the photos, which just seems cruel for a cat with that much fur. \n\nBut, um, what were we talking about? Oh, right, eating when I'm down. Yeah, no, it doesn't really work like that for me. I don't notice the eating helping me feel better or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating in response to any emotional feelings, such as sadness or anxiety, when these episodes happen?\n\nPatient: I know we just kind of touched on this, but no, I really don't feel like it's from being sad or anxious. I just haven't noticed any emotional trigger like that. \n\nHonestly, if you want to see real anxiety, you should watch people use the self-checkout machines at my store. It is wild. It's like the second they step up to the screen, they completely forget how to read. This one guy yesterday was trying to buy a bag of Honeycrisp apples, but he kept aggressively tapping the button for bananas. And the machine is just blaring, \"Please place your item in the bagging area,\" over and over, and he's just staring at the scanner like it's an alien spaceship. He was sweating profusely, like, dripping sweat onto the glass. I had to walk over with my little barcode card and override the system, and he looked at me like I had just defused a bomb. \n\nHe actually tried to tip me a dollar, which we aren't even allowed to accept because corporate considers it a \"conflict of interest.\" They made us watch this whole two-hour training video about bribery last month, which is hilarious because who is bribing a grocery store cashier? Like, what kind of underground produce smuggling ring do they think we're running out of lane four? The video had these terrible actors in oversized suits sliding envelopes of cash across a breakroom table. \n\nBut, uh, wait, sorry. What was the question again? Oh, right, my emotions. Yeah, no, I don't really notice any sadness or anxiety causing the eating. It just doesn't feel connected to my mood.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any connection between your eating episodes and a decrease in your interest in sex?\n\nPatient: Sex? Um, no, I haven't really noticed any change there, or any connection to the eating stuff. I mean, things are pretty much the same as they've always been in that department. \n\nThough, honestly, dating right now is just... it's a nightmare. I went on this date last week with a woman I met on one of those apps. She suggested we meet at this artisanal coffee shop downtown, the kind where the baristas wear leather aprons and look like they're judging your life choices. I ordered a regular drip coffee, and the guy behind the counter actually sighed. Like, a full, heavy sigh, as if I had just insulted his ancestors. \n\nAnyway, my date shows up, and she brings her dog. Which, fine, I like dogs, but this was a Great Dane. It was practically the size of a horse. And she hadn't mentioned it at all. We sat outside because, obviously, you can't bring a pony into a coffee shop, and this dog spent the entire hour trying to steal my blueberry scone. At one point, it rested its massive head on my knee, and the drool—it was like a faucet. My jeans were completely soaked. And she just kept talking about her ex-boyfriend's podcast about competitive thumb wrestling. Apparently, it's a very cutthroat community. She didn't even seem to notice that her dog was actively dissolving my pants. \n\nSo, yeah, dating is exhausting. But, uh, to answer your question, no, I haven't noticed any decrease in my interest in sex or anything like that. It doesn't seem related to the eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control what or how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's... that's actually exactly what brought me in. It's like, some nights after work, or maybe if I've had a really tense dinner, I'll start eating and it just feels like the decision-making part of my brain completely shuts off. I can literally see myself reaching for more food, but I just can't make myself stop. It's the weirdest feeling. \n\nSpeaking of tense dinners, though, I went to my sister's house for dinner on Sunday, and her kids are just... they're menaces. She has these twin boys, they're seven, and they have decided that their new favorite game is \"Floor is Lava,\" but like, an extreme version where they hurl themselves off the furniture like stuntmen. One of them, Liam, launched himself off the back of the sofa and landed squarely in the middle of the coffee table. It didn't break, miraculously, but he knocked over a full pitcher of iced tea. It went everywhere. And my sister didn't even blink! She just kept talking about her new air fryer like she was filming an infomercial. She was telling me how you can make a hard-boiled egg in it, which doesn't even make sense because it's not boiling if there's no water, right? It's just a hot egg. I tried to explain the physics of that to her, but she just waved me off and told me I was being closed-minded about modern appliances. \n\nAnd meanwhile, I'm sitting there with iced tea seeping into my socks, watching her kids try to scale the curtains. \n\nBut, um, anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, right, controlling the eating. Yeah, no, I definitely feel like I can't stop once I start. It's like I'm on autopilot or something.\n\nDoctor: Do you consistently feel guilty immediately after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's almost immediate. The second I stop, I just feel this overwhelming guilt, like I've done something terrible. It's a really awful feeling. \n\nActually, speaking of awful feelings, you know what else makes me feel terrible? The breakroom at my store. It's just... it's a disaster zone. We have this one guy, Gary, who works in the deli, and he insists on microwaving fish for lunch every single Tuesday. And not just any fish, it's like this heavily seasoned, pungent tilapia that lingers in the air for hours. It gets into the upholstery of the one sad little couch we have in there. And the fridge! Don't even get me started on the fridge. It's like a science experiment. Someone left a container of yogurt in there last month, and I swear it's started to grow hair. I tried to bring it up to management, but they just put up a passive-aggressive sign that says, \"Your mother doesn't work here, clean up after yourself.\" Which is so unhelpful because clearly, the sign isn't working. I've started eating my lunch in my car, which isn't great either because it's been so hot lately. I had to crack the windows yesterday, and this huge bee flew in and got trapped against the windshield. I spent ten minutes trying to coax it out with a receipt. \n\nBut, uh, wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the guilt. Yeah, the guilt is definitely there. It hits me really hard right after I finish eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, we talked about this a little bit already, but yeah. That's actually the main reason I made this appointment. It's like... some nights, usually after a long shift at the store or if I've had one of those tense dinners we talked about, I'll start eating and it's like the decision-making part of my brain just completely shuts off. I can literally watch my hand reaching for more food, but I just can't make myself stop. It's so frustrating. \n\nSpeaking of frustrating, you know what happened at the store yesterday? The barcode scanner at my register just decided to give up on life. Right in the middle of the evening rush. And of course, it was when I had this customer who was buying, I kid you not, thirty cans of cat food. All different flavors. And because the scanner died, I had to type in the little numbers under the barcode by hand for every single one. The guy behind her in line was huffing and puffing like a steam engine, looking at his watch every five seconds. I wanted to tell him, \"Look, buddy, I didn't break the scanner on purpose,\" but you know, customer service voice. You just have to smile and apologize. And then the cat food lady started arguing with me about a coupon that expired in 2019. She insisted that because the store was closed for a remodel back then for two weeks, we should honor it now. The logic was baffling. I had to call the manager over, and he just gave it to her anyway to make her leave, which always makes me look like the bad guy. \n\nBut, um, anyway, yeah. The eating thing. It's exactly like that. I just feel like I have zero control once I get started. Like I'm completely on autopilot and I can't snap out of it."
